Proven in `tests/integration/packaging.test.ts`. **And the waste & sustainability report** (`services/inventory/src/waste.ts`, M28-FR-04): `POST /v1/waste/records/:id` + `POST /v1/waste/coverage` log valued waste and the expected reporting units, and `GET /v1/waste/report` + `…/compare` carry COVERAGE on the face of the number — `not_comparable` below 80% with the silent departments named, a comparison that refuses to call a fall an improvement when coverage moved. A store-staff record vs an accountant read split. Proven in `tests/integration/waste-reporting.test.ts`. **And cold-chain assessment** (`services/inventory/src/cold-chain.ts`, M10-FR-02): `POST /v1/quality/cold-chain/assess` runs `assessColdChain` — duration counts as much as peak, a batch with no reading fails rather than passes, and a breach quarantines automatically; deliberately stateless (no second temperature store — facilities-monitoring owns that truth, P-02). Proven in `tests/integration/quality-cold-chain.test.ts` |
| `services/identity` — API-01 | ~120 lines | **Built.** Scope resolution, maker-checker grants, no credential stored anywhere |
| `services/compliance` — API-11 | ~130 lines, 4 tests | **Built and persisting (M34-FR-03).** The licence/certificate/obligation register on the tested `packages/compliance` engine — a complete register nothing on the cloud fed until now. `POST /v1/compliance/obligations/:id` registers a licence/scale-stamping/food-licence with its **named** responsible person and expiry (append-only — a change is a new fact, never an overwrite), `GET /v1/compliance/alerts` reads what needs attention **worst-first** (escalating; an expired one keeps shouting), and `GET /v1/compliance/status` says whether the shop is compliant. Subsumes the ratified R2 **B7** (weighing re-verification alerts) and **B10** (FSSAI-licence alerts) — a scale stamping and a food licence are both obligations. Gated `compliance.obligation.manage`/`.read` (owner/store-manager). Proven in `tests/integration/compliance-obligations.test.ts` |
| `services/platform` — API-11 | ~170 lines | **Built.** Health that distinguishes alive from working; time-bound support access. **Now also hosts facilities schedules** (`services/platform/src/facilities.ts`): cleaning/pest/fire/electrical/statutory tasks where a tick without required evidence is refused, a self-verified safety check is refused (§28), and a compliance-linked miss escalates by itself while cleaning never does. Proven in `tests/integration/facilities-schedules.test.ts`. **And the integration gateway** (`services/platform/src/integration.ts`, M32-FR-04): `POST /v1/integration/matrix/:id` + `…/adapters/:id` register the certified matrix and adapters — a payment adapter retaining card data or a literal credential refused with no override, an uncertified/non-RBI provider refused — `GET …/devices/check` names a certified alternative, and `GET /v1/integration/health` catches an adapter silent for days while `posUnaffected` stays true. Proven in `tests/integration/integration-gateway.test.ts`. **And inbound signed webhooks** (`services/platform/src/webhooks.ts`, M32-FR-01): `POST /v1/integration/webhooks/:provider/deliveries/:id` runs `verifyWebhook` — a forged or wrong-tenant delivery is a 401 security event, a stale one 422, a duplicate a 200 provider-retry ack, the HMAC key resolved from a vault ref. Proven in `tests/integration/webhooks.test.ts`. **And connector mapping validation** (`services/platform/src/connectors.ts`, M32-FR-02): `POST /v1/integration/connectors/:id/mappings/:version/validate` runs `applyMapping` — an unmapped field, a lookup miss or a missing required field is named rather than silently run. Proven in `tests/integration/connector-mapping.test.ts`. **And backup verification & restore reconciliation** (`services/platform/src/backup-verification.ts`, M35-FR-01/02, P-04): `POST /v1/platform/backups/verify` runs `verifyBackup` — an empty artefact, a checksum that no longer matches, no control totals, not-encrypted or no off-site copy each make it not usable — and `POST …/reconcile-restore` runs `reconcileRestore` where every row count and money total must match EXACTLY (no "close enough"), a single difference naming the table and what it is worth; stateless. Gated `backup.verify.read`. Proven in `tests/integration/backup-verification.test.ts`. **And the branch open/close lifecycle** (`services/platform/src/branch-lifecycle.ts`, M01-FR-04): `POST /v1/platform/branches/transition/evaluate` runs `evaluateTransition` — measured readiness, every blocker returned at once (stock/cash/open-docs/unsent-sync/exceptions), owner approval required with the approver ≠ the requester (§28), a permanent close over unsent sync refused because it would destroy legitimately-made sales (§31); stateless and non-mutating. Gated `branch.transition.evaluate`. Proven in `tests/integration/branch-lifecycle.test.ts`. **And the versioned document-template register** (`services/platform/src/documents.ts`, M31-FR-01/M36-FR-02): `POST /v1/documents/templates/:id/publish` runs `publishTemplateVersion` — a template change is a NEW version, never an overwrite (there is no edit path), needs a change note and a separate approver (§28), branding frozen into the version — and `GET …/current?at=` runs `currentVersion` (the version in force at a moment, so July's invoice keeps July's layout); event-sourced (append-only `TemplateVersionPublished`). Gated `document.template.manage`/`.read`. Proven in `tests/integration/documents-templates.test.ts` |
| `services/purchase` — API-03 | ~200 lines | **Built and persisting.** A supplier invoice's lines are now **captured on the cloud surface** — `POST /v1/purchase/invoices/:invoiceId/capture` writes `PurchaseInvoiceCaptured`, and the match folds exactly those lines — so `/match` answers against real data instead of an empty stream that refused every invoice; the capture is idempotent per invoice, so a re-send collapses rather than doubling what the supplier is owed. Three-way match pays the lowest of three and holds back the difference, and an invoice nobody captured is refused as *not checked* — a different answer from *checked and clean*. Bank change verified out of band, dated, and recorded as an event rather than an overwrite. What is on order now answers with a real figure once POs exist (M06-WIRE): `POST /v1/purchase/orders/:id` **proposes** a PO (requisitioner = the authenticated buyer) and `POST …/approval` **issues** it under a second person (§28, refusing a self-approval and a blocked supplier), and `GET /v1/purchase/commitments` runs the tested `computeOpenCommitment` over the issued POs — still *not known* (not zero) until the first PO is issued. (Receipt/cancellation netting of the open figure and supplier scorecards remain engine-level.) The match rule itself lives in `packages/purchasing/src/three-way-match.ts` and is re-exported here, so the buyer's browser screen runs the **same** implementation as the service rather than a copy of it. (The buyer's ERP screen captures into the **offline store-edge pack**; this endpoint is the **cloud** counterpart — the edge→cloud sync of captures is a separate wire.) **Now also hosts the supplier portal** (`services/purchase/src/supplier-portal.ts`): nothing a supplier submits takes effect on its own — catalogue/RFQ/claim land requiresReview, an ASN/invoice needs the grant + compliance, another supplier's order is refused, a retry is a duplicate; the partner's grants come from stored config, never the payload. **Compliance is checked AT THE ACTION** (M24-FR-03): the partner's real documents (with expiry + verification) sit in its config and `checkPartnerCompliance` runs at the submission, so an expired/missing/unverified required document blocks the very next ASN/invoice while an expiring one only warns, and `GET …/compliance?asOf=` shows a buyer why. **Also hosts the supplier statement** (M24-FR-04): `POST …/statement/opening` + `…/statement/lines/:ref` record the opening and signed lines, and `GET …/statement` runs `buildStatement` — a closing balance from named buckets cross-checked (`reconciles`), a dispute shown separately, and a login without `view_statement` answered with a permission answer not a zero balance. Proven end to end in `tests/integration/purchase-capture-match.test.ts`, `tests/integration/supplier-portal.test.ts`, `tests/integration/supplier-portal-compliance.test.ts` and `tests/integration/supplier-portal-statement.test.ts` |
| `services/finance` — API-09 | ~200 lines | **Built and persisting.** A closed period is closed — and open is the *absence of a close*, folded from the stream, so no period can be open and closed at once. Journals, closes and who posted into a month are all real. **Control totals are not**: no genuine second source exists inside this API, so the close refuses rather than closing unchecked. **Now also hosts settlement** (`services/finance/src/settlement.ts`): a provider batch is refused unless it reconciles to its own declared figures and is not a duplicate (`POST /v1/settlement/batches`), and the day's card/UPI tenders are reviewed against what the provider paid, telling apart not-due-yet from late and valuing short/over/unknown (`GET /v1/settlement/review`); the review reports the window it read, never silently calling an out-of-window tender settled. An exception then becomes an **owned, evidenced, outcome-closed investigation** (`POST /v1/settlement/investigations`, `…/:id/evidence`, `…/:id/resolve`, `GET …/investigations`) — a mutable aggregate folded from its events, with a §28 write-off control and ageing buckets. **And B2B credit control** (`services/finance/src/b2b-credit.ts`): a per-customer credit limit and an AR balance projected from invoices/payments, with an over-limit order or expired contract blocked pending a separate approver (§28). **And B2B collections** (`services/finance/src/b2b-collections.ts`, M22-FR-04): structured receivables aged from the DUE DATE, a payment allocated oldest-due-first with the overpayment held unapplied (settled projected from the allocations), a disputed invoice never chaseable, and dunning that recommends stop-supply for a person to commit — proven in `tests/integration/b2b-collections.test.ts`. **And B2B commission** (`services/finance/src/b2b-commission.ts`, M22-FR-03): a salesperson's commission EARNED not stated — `POST /v1/b2b/commissions/:sp/accruals/:id` computes the payout on the server with the exact-money engine (half-up, cap after the round), `GET …/:sp` reports the total projected from the accruals never a stored balance, a re-send collapses on the accrual id — proven in `tests/integration/b2b-commission.test.ts`. **And the B2B document chain, part 1** (`services/finance/src/b2b-documents.ts`, M22-FR-02): a quotation issued on its OWN gap-free series drawn ONCE and only on success (a rejected quotation leaves no gap — the engine validates without a number, and a number is allocated only if it would issue), then converted into a sales order at the QUOTED price through the REAL credit gate (`checkCredit`: no account → blocked, over-limit → blocked, sufficient → cleared) and refusing a second conversion of the same quote — proven in `tests/integration/b2b-documents-chain.test.ts`; and part 2 — a proforma (never tax-claimable), a challan carrying what LEFT with over-delivery refused, the tax invoice built from the CHALLANS (partial delivery bills partially, an over-invoice refused), and the chain check naming delivered-but-not-invoiced — proven in `tests/integration/b2b-documents-chain-2.test.ts`. **And concession** (`services/finance/src/concession.ts`): a partner's counter charged rent/revenue-share/higher-of-both exactly, settled with the tills' takings as a **liability never revenue**, deposit stated-not-netted, till-vs-counter difference a valued exception. **And scrap** (`services/finance/src/scrap.ts`): scrap/recycling proceeds made to EXIST — recorded not refused, off-books cash and rate drift flagged, posted-to-finance clears the flag. Proven in `tests/integration/settlement-cash-office.test.ts`, `tests/integration/settlement-investigations.test.ts`, `tests/integration/b2b-credit.test.ts`, `tests/integration/concession.test.ts` and `tests/integration/scrap.test.ts`. **And refund exceptions & day totals** (`services/finance/src/refund-exceptions.ts`, M14-FR-03/04): `POST /v1/finance/refunds/exceptions` runs `refundExceptions` — a refund pays money OUT, so a failed reversal (customer owed now) and an over-refund (paid out more than taken) are surfaced worst-first, each valued and owned (P-03) — and `POST …/day-totals` states confirmed/uncertain/failed separately so a manager sees how much money is in an unknown state; stateless (the reversals live in settlement/POS). Gated `refund.exception.read`. Proven in `tests/integration/refund-exceptions.test.ts`. **And GST e-invoicing** (`services/finance/src/e-invoice.ts`, A20, on the tested `packages/e-invoice` engine): `POST /v1/finance/e-invoice/eligibility` (₹5-cr-gated, B2C-excluded), `…/build` (canonical IRP request from a Rule-46-valid invoice only, with the IRP idempotency key), `…/apply-irp-result` (turns the IRP's answer into the stored record, **never fabricating the IRN/QR**, `unknown` kept as its own state); stateless deterministic core, gated `finance.einvoice.generate`. **And the durable lifecycle store** (`services/finance/src/e-invoice-register.ts` + `eInvoiceAdapter`, A20 inc2, event-sourced per invoice): `POST …/invoices/:id/submit` → `…/record-response` (where the GSP connector posts the IRP's answer; a registered IRN is FINAL) → `…/cancel` (within the 24h window, else a credit note) → `GET …/invoices/:id`; the credentialed GSP connector that makes the live IRP call is the deployment step. Proven in `tests/integration/e-invoice.test.ts` and `tests/integration/e-invoice-lifecycle.test.ts`. **And the GST-returns write path** (`services/finance/src/gst-returns.ts` + `gstReturnsAdapter`, A5): `POST /v1/finance/outward-supplies/:documentId` PERSISTS a document's tax lines (HSN/taxable/CGST-SGST-IGST) append-only — a free-text HSN rejected before storage, a B2B needs a recipient GSTIN, an inconsistent tax split refused, idempotent per document — and `GET /v1/finance/gstr1/table-12?from=&to=` folds the stored lines into the HSN-wise GSTR-1 Table 12 with the B2B/B2C split, while `GET /v1/finance/gstr1/return?from=&to=` assembles the full return (B2B invoice-level + B2C rate-wise + HSN, all from the same stored lines) and `GET /v1/finance/gstr1/export?from=&to=&gstin=&fp=` serialises it into the GSTN portal JSON (paise→rupees, dates reformatted, grouped by `ctin`); gated `finance.gstr.generate`/`.read`. Proven in `tests/integration/gst-returns.test.ts` |
| `services/reporting` — API-10 | ~110 lines | **Built.** Read-only; no figure without its as-at; not-available never rendered as zero. **Now also hosts the owner alerts inbox** (`services/reporting/src/owner-alerts.ts`, M29-FR-03): `POST /v1/reporting/owner-alerts` runs `raiseOwnerAlerts` — control by exception (P-03), raw exceptions GROUPED by kind/branch/person into the few alerts an owner reads (six voids are one conversation), each keeping every transaction id for the drill-through, every threshold the owner's own, an unset kind raising nothing; stateless and read-only (nothing commits). Gated `owner.alert.read`. Proven in `tests/integration/owner-alerts.test.ts` |
| `services/customer` — API-06 | ~140 lines | **Built and persisting.** Consent decided at use, with given/withdrawn/given-again all kept — the middle one is what a regulator asks about. **Loyalty points are now wired**: `POST /v1/customers/:id/points` earns/burns/reverses money-like append-only movements, the balance is projected (never stored) and a burn can never take it below zero; a customer with no movement still answers *not known* rather than zero, so the distinction survives the fold. **Gift cards / store credit are wired too** (`services/customer/src/stored-value.ts`): a card is issued with an opening value, redeemed never-overdrawn / expiry-refused / offline-capped / idempotent, and its balance is projected from movements (no setter anywhere) — a liability, not a figure. Proven in `tests/integration/loyalty-points.test.ts` and `tests/integration/stored-value.test.ts`. **And the notification send guard** (`services/customer/src/notification-guard.ts`, M31-FR-03): `POST /v1/notifications/can-send` runs `canNotify` — consent-safe by construction, the gates in order (approved template → suppression → consent+frequency → budget), a breach BLOCKS never warns and the first failing gate is named; stateless (the caller holds the contact's consent). Gated `notification.send.check`. Proven in `tests/integration/notification-guard.test.ts` |
| `services/orders` — API-07 | ~150 lines | **Built and persisting.** Promise and reserve in one call; short lines told before payment. On-hand comes from the **inventory service's own projection**, deliberately not a second one — the function that promises stock to a customer must not drift from the one that counts it |
| `services/fulfilment` — API-08 | ~160 lines | **Built and persisting.** Every attempt recorded; proof never deleted; cash settled per driver per run — and now attempts against orders **not on the run** are reported too. The dispatch list itself is not on this surface yet (M20) |
| `services/migration` — API-12 | ~120 lines | **Built.** Refuses a production target on every request; serves the verification report |
| `services/ai` — API-13 | ~170 lines | **Built.** Proposals only; kill switch needs no approval; budget checked before the call |
| `services/` remaining domain services | **none** | **All thirteen APIs are served.** API-01…13 each have a service; see the surface gate below |
| `services/api` — composition root | ~220 lines | **Built.** Config checked at boot, store opened, fourteen service modules (thirteen APIs — `pricing` is a second module under API-02) on one router **built exactly once**, drains on SIGTERM. **All persist.** Tokens are verified against the configured identity provider; `services/api/src/roles.ts` holds the role catalogue, because a role is configuration and its holders are tenant data |
| `infra/` | compose (db · migrate · **api** · web), Dockerfile, CI deploy job | **Built.** One command brings the whole stack up on one machine; CI builds the image, proves it refuses a bad configuration, and brings the stack to ready. The database password the deploy step generates is **URL-safe** (hex, never base64 — a `/` in the userinfo makes `DATABASE_URL` an invalid URL and migrate exits 1), guarded by `tests/guardrails/the-db-password-is-url-safe.test.ts` |

**The consequence, stated plainly so it cannot be missed (reconciled 7 Aug 2026 against the audit):**
the business rules are finished and the system is **only partially assembled**. An earlier version of
this paragraph said "there is no service layer" — that is now **stale**: a thin service layer exists
and all thirteen APIs persist to PostgreSQL (see the table above). But two facts qualify every
"Built" below and must be read with it:

1. **The services are still thinning out — but measurably less thin.** Early on, six of the seven
   domain services were kernel-only re-implementations that did **not** import the rich tested engines
   in `packages/`. The **CORE-01…05** wiring workstream has been collapsing that gap one reviewed
   increment at a time — fulfilment, ai, reporting, finance (credit-notes, notes), stored-value/loyalty,
   returns, purchase, and now **compliance** all run their `packages/` engine on the live API.
   **5 of 79 packages are now imported only by their own tests** (down from 35) — and the CORE-05
   wiring workstream is **COMPLETE**: **every genuine domain engine is now on the live API.** The five
   that remain are shared libraries or frontend that by design do not sit behind a service route
   (`types`, `utils`, `config`, `ui`, `api-client`) — a type package or a UI-component package has no
   endpoint to serve. The ten engines wired across CORE-05 were `compliance`, `quality`, `self-checkout`,
   `owner-control`, `reversal`, `notifications`, `ops`, `org`, `documents` and `suspended-sales`.
   (`packages/e-invoice` and `packages/restricted-sales` are new packages, each added and wired in the
   same increment — for A20 e-invoicing and B14 tobacco restrictions — never test-only, so the total is
   now 79 packages with the same 5 unwired-by-design.)
2. **"Built" ≠ end-to-end.** A requirement row reading *Built* means its rules are implemented and
   unit-proved. It does **not** mean a cashier can scan an item today, nor that the row is
   *WIRED / INTEGRATION TESTED / E2E VERIFIED* in the sense of the completion vocabulary below.

The ASSEMBLY / WIRING workstream (below) tracks each module through that vocabulary. This section is
the honest counterpart to the family table above and must be read beside it.

## Completion status vocabulary (adopted 7 August 2026)

Per owner instruction, "engine implemented and unit-tested" is **not** "module complete." Every module
is now tracked through this ladder; a module is COMPLETE only when the whole chain — requirement →
domain engine → application/service → API/event contract → persistence → authorization → UI/channel
where applicable → integration tests → E2E → operational evidence — is connected and verified.

`NOT STARTED` · `ENGINE ONLY` · `PARTIALLY WIRED` · `WIRED` · `INTEGRATION TESTED` · `E2E VERIFIED` ·
`UAT VERIFIED` · `PRODUCTION VERIFIED` · `EXTERNALLY BLOCKED`

Never report `ENGINE ONLY`, simulated, synthetic, mocked, or documented work as product completion.
The evidence each rung requires is the mandatory **`docs/MODULE-COMPLETION-TEMPLATE.md`**, copied into
every module-assembly PR; a module moves up only to the highest rung whose boxes are ticked. This is
**CI-enforced**: `tests/guardrails/completion-ladder-has-evidence.test.ts` fails the build if any
module below claims `WIRED`+ without registered evidence that exists (and `INTEGRATION TESTED`+ must
point at a real integration/e2e test), so an engine-only module cannot be labelled complete. The
shared E2E harness (`tests/support/api-harness.ts`) and local/test IdP (`tests/support/local-idp.ts`)
are how `E2E VERIFIED` is proved — through the real surface, real token verifier and real per-tenant
authorization, the way `main()` composes them.

| M13 | Tender/settlement | PARTIALLY WIRED (**M13-FR-01/FR-03 cloud refund guard WIRED+integration-tested 7 Aug** — at-most-once against the whole history + refund cap + §28 approver + card-pending, on the live API; tender capture and settlement/recon (M14) still off the cloud surface) | M31 | Documents/numbering | PARTIALLY WIRED (gap-free numbering + versioned template register + issue/reproduce with frozen-content reproduction live on API-11; batch re-issue + archival follow-on) |
| M14 | Settlement/recon | PARTIALLY WIRED (**M14-FR-03 batch import + day review + investigation lifecycle WIRED+integration-tested 7 Aug** — provider file refused unless it reconciles to its own figures/not a duplicate; card/UPI tenders reviewed with not-due-yet vs late ageing and short/over/unknown valued; an exception becomes an owned, evidenced, outcome-closed case with a §28 write-off control and ageing buckets; **M14-FR-01 till cash WIRED+integration-tested 7 Aug** — float/loan/pickup/safe-drop/return with one custodian and no overdraw, drawer balance and custodian projected; **M14-FR-02 blind till count / shift close WIRED+integration-tested 7 Aug** — expected vs blind count, over/short valued, a material variance needs a reason and is raised for reconciliation, on the live API. The cloud cash-office trio FR-01/02/03 is now wired; only the store-edge trading-day lock (M14-FR-04, a manager-screen/edge control) sits outside the cloud API by design) | M32 | Integration gateway | PARTIALLY WIRED (**M32-FR-04 certified matrix + adapter registration + integration health WIRED+integration-tested 7 Aug** — a payment adapter retaining card data or a literal credential REFUSED with no override, an uncertified/non-RBI provider refused, a device refusal NAMES the certified alternative, and health is measured by when it last WORKED so a quietly-silent adapter is caught while the till stays unaffected (`posUnaffected` typed true); on the live API; **M32-FR-01 inbound signed-webhook RECEIPT WIRED+integration-tested 7 Aug** — a forged or wrong-tenant delivery is a 401 security event, a stale one 422, a duplicate a 200 provider-retry ack not an attack. Versioned-request/idempotency (FR-01) is served by the kernel + the Stage-19 gate; **M32-FR-02 connector mapping VALIDATION WIRED+integration-tested 7 Aug** — an unmapped field/lookup-miss/missing-required named rather than silently run. **M32-FR-03 managed secrets now WIRED too (M32-WIRE)** — the credential lifecycle on API-11 holding NO secret material (a vault REFERENCE, never the key; a raw value in vaultRef is refused): register a secret reference, ROTATE with an overlap (the previous version stays valid for a grace period so unsynced edge devices keep working; a no-grace rotation is refused — that is a revocation), REVOKE immediately for a compromise and NAME what breaks, and REVIEW the inventory (overdue/never-rotated + a revoked-secret-still-referenced or sandbox-in-production as the blocking findings, phrased by what the secret protects); event-sourced SecretStateRecorded, restart-safe, integration-tested (managed-secrets.test.ts, 5 cases). Gated platform.setup.write/read (owner-scoped). The connector DELIVERY/dead-letter (FR-02) and usage-signal monitoring (findUsageSignals) remain engine-only worker/transport concerns) |

Integration-tested end to end) | M34 | Compliance/retention | PARTIALLY WIRED (primitives; **risk-register + gate-blocking FR-04 core wired** — an open critical risk blocks its QG until accepted in a named person's own name with a written reason; incident/remediation/control-health legs remain) |

Partner FR-04 still engine-only) |

**Summary (module level, 36):** 1 E2E VERIFIED · 2 INTEGRATION TESTED · 11 WIRED · 22 PARTIALLY WIRED ·
0 ENGINE ONLY · 0 NOT STARTED (but see the restored D-FR rows below). No module is UAT- or
PRODUCTION-verified. _These counts are derived from the ladder rungs above and cross-checked against
`docs/completion-status.json` by `tests/guardrails/the-module-ladder-matches-the-ledger.test.ts`._ The programme drives each row rightward, engine-into-service, with authz, tenant
isolation, idempotency, persistence, audit, offline/sync, contracts, integration + E2E tests, UI
states, and English/Tamil at every increment.

## Design artifacts (Stage 3–4)

Store-Core requirements are now covered at **design level** by the artifacts below; the
individual rows keep `Design → docs/requirements/M##.md` as their requirement anchor and
stay **In design** until code + tests exist (Definition of Done). These are the design
references those rows resolve to:

| Artifact | Location | Covers |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Screen specs (all 14 §27 surfaces) | `docs/design/screens/` + `docs/design/design-system.md` | UX/QG-02 for every role surface |
| Architecture overview | `docs/architecture/README.md` | §19 planes, bounded contexts → API-01…13 |
| Data model (§29) | `docs/architecture/data-model.md` | entity/rule model, one-commerce-truth |
| API & event catalogue (§30) | `docs/api/catalogue.md` | API-01…13, conventions, §30.2 events |
| Offline-sync design (§31) | `docs/architecture/offline-sync.md` | P-01 detail, sync protocol, conflicts |
| Migration design (§34) | `docs/architecture/migration-design.md` | MG-01…12 pipeline, QG-07 control totals |
| Count verification (§34, OB-06, MG-06, §28) | `packages/migration/src/count-verification.ts` | Proving the extracted stock against the shelves — the only thing that can. **Value-stratified**, not random: the high-value lines are a CENSUS, the tail is sampled, because a random sample that happens to miss the ghee has verified almost nothing while looking thorough. Refuses a sample chosen by whoever ran the extraction (§28); the expected quantity never reaches the count sheet (`expectedQtyShownToCounter: false`); the draw is seeded so an auditor can ask why THIS line. The extrapolation is labelled an **estimate** and returns `undefined` rather than a fabricated figure when the sample cannot support one. Tested in `tests/unit/migration-count-verification.test.ts` |
| Verification report (§34, OB-06, MG-06, QG-07, P-08) | `packages/migration/src/verification-report.ts` | The one page the owner and the CA sign, gathering all six checks into the language of the runbook. The most dangerous file in the migration, because **a report is what people believe** — nobody re-derives a figure once a signed page exists — so the refusals are about the document rather than the arithmetic. **It cannot be produced from some of the domains**: four of twelve renders perfectly, every heading filled, and is a complete-looking account of a third of the business, so nothing renders until every domain has a finding. **An unproved domain is a row in the same table in the same type**, never a footnote (P-08). **A proved domain that states no limit is refused** — every check has a real limit, and a blank is read by whoever signs as "no limit". **Each exception is accepted one at a time, by name, by the owner, with a reason**, and a reason that fills the box without saying anything is refused as `packages/migration/src/cutover.ts` refuses a non-explanation. Whoever ran the extraction can neither prepare nor sign it (QG-07). Signatures accumulate and are never replaced — a signature belongs to the figures that were on the page when it was given. Renders Indian money grouping, every witness rather than the first, and counts where the domain is not a rupee total. Worked example in `docs/evidence/example-verification-report.md`; tested in `tests/unit/migration-verification-report.test.ts` |
| **OB-06 verification gate** (§34, OB-06, MG-06, QG-07) | `tests/integration/every-figure-has-a-witness.test.ts` | All six outside-evidence checks run as **one pass over one shop**, against real PostgreSQL 16.13. Adds the two things unit tests structurally cannot: it **walks the `VERIFIES` table for all twelve domains and proves every named source has a module behind it** — catching a domain with a named witness and no code, which is what a migration under time pressure produces; and it **proves the witnesses agree with each other**, tying four figures across checks (bank gross = filed taxable + tax; signed Stock = the counted shelves; signed Creditors = the suppliers' own confirmations; signed GST payable = the filed return), so a wrong number must be wrong consistently in two independent records to survive. The gate is proved to **fail**: withhold the physical count and five domains are refused by name as `verified_by_the_same_system`. The structural check is enforced by `Record<ExternalSource, string>`, so adding a kind of evidence with no check built is a **compile error**, not merely a red test. Verified figures are banked as append-only events carrying the witness that proved them. 16 assertions |
| Loyalty verification (§34, OB-06, MG-06, M12, §28) | `packages/migration/src/loyalty-verification.ts` | The last of the six and the odd one out: **no third party keeps a record of a customer's points**, so the only witness is the customer. Two facts about customers decide the design. **The feedback is asymmetric, so complaints are a useless sample** — a customer whose points fell complains, one whose points rose never does, so a sample drawn from complaints is 100% shortfalls by construction and would confirm a systematically over-crediting migration with real evidence from real customers. **Refused by name.** And **the loud direction is not the dangerous one**: understated points are visible, immediate and self-correcting because they generate complaints; overstated points are silent, permanent, redeemed in goods at our cost, and never discovered — so the silent side sorts first, is valued at the tenant's own cost per redeemed point, and is never netted against the loud side. The central control mirrors the stock count: **the balance is never shown to the customer** (`balanceShownToTheCustomer: false`), because asked *"is it 450?"* almost anybody says yes — what is accepted is the customer's own figure or the activity they remember. Sampling is seeded and three-strata (largest balances, tier boundaries, random tail), with the census winning over the boundary rule so coverage is never double-counted. Tier changes are named — a tier is on every receipt. `provesTheBalanceWasEarned` is typed as the literal `false`. Tested in `tests/unit/migration-loyalty-verification.test.ts` |
| Books verification (§34, OB-06, MG-06, M16) | `packages/migration/src/books-verification.ts` | The signed closing balance sheet **is** the opening position, so this ties the other five checks together — stock, debtors, creditors, cash and tax all appear on it, each already proved by its own evidence. Also, stated plainly, **the weakest of the six as independent evidence**: the CA prepared them from the same old system, so what they add is a professional signature and the discipline of double entry, not an independent source (`provesTheAccountsAreRight: false`). **A balancing figure is refused by name** — Suspense, Opening Difference, Diff A/c, plug — because posting the difference there makes the books balance and be wrong, and that account is never cleared. Checked **before** the balance test, so a book that closes only because of the plug is never reported as balancing. Also refused: draft (unsigned) accounts; accounts that do not themselves balance; a cutover that is not the day after the accounts' period end (out by a whole trading period while looking authoritative); and the absence of what **only the CA has** — depreciation, provisions, accruals, prepayments, drawings — whose absence is not a variance but exactly the amount that would end up in suspense. Contra accounts (drawings, accumulated depreciation) are known, so the wrong-side flag does not fire on every correct set of books. Tested in `tests/unit/migration-books-verification.test.ts` |
| Tax verification (§34, OB-06, MG-06, M23) | `packages/migration/src/tax-verification.ts` | The only check that runs **backwards**: a filed return is true as a matter of law, so the question is not whether it agrees with the books but what the books must become. Four refusals, each load-bearing: **no acknowledgement reference** — a spreadsheet named after a return is a working paper, and the ARN is the only thing separating filed from prepared; **not a whole tax period** — a part period is short by design and reads as missing sales; **superseded by an amendment** — reconciling to the restated original gives a wrong answer with a flawless audit trail; **the return's own arithmetic failing** — reconciling to it would spread the error through every opening figure. **A slab rate is never inferred from a total**: an average across a mixed basket is not a rate anything could have been sold at, so a books line off the statutory slabs is refused, and no entry point taking a total and a rate exists (proven by scanning the module's real exports). GSTR-1 is checked against GSTR-3B as well as against the books, because the department reconciles those two automatically — a difference there is **inherited, not created**. A difference against a filed return is a **disclosure to the CA**, not a private data fix. `provesTaxWasCorrectlyCharged` is typed as the literal `false`. Tested in `tests/unit/migration-tax-verification.test.ts` |
| Extraction tool (§34, OB-06, MG-01/02/05) | `scripts/extract-check.mts` | **The door.** Every verification module in `packages/migration` was unreachable by anybody not writing TypeScript; this takes a file exactly as the incumbent produced it and runs the checks in the runbook's order. **Completeness runs first and refuses** — a paginated grid that exported one page parses perfectly, sums to its own total to the paisa, and holds a tenth of the shop, so nothing else looks at the file until that is satisfied. Two defects found by pointing it at a realistic file: it split on the separator directly, and **Indian money is written with commas while these exports are comma-separated**, so `"1,28,000.00"` became three cells and only the figures that matter were destroyed — while the row count still looked right; and it added one to `headerLine`, which the parser already numbers 1-based so a person can quote it. Run as a real subprocess in `tests/integration/the-extraction-tool-runs.test.ts`, because a tool tested only through its library is a tool whose arguments, exit codes and output nobody has checked. Procedure: `docs/runbooks/extraction-work-plan.md` |
| Banking verification (§34, OB-06, MG-06, §29.1) | `packages/migration/src/banking-verification.ts` | Sales proved against the bank, which has no interest in agreeing with our old ERP. Neither `packages/reconciliation` (needs a shared provider reference) nor `packages/settlement` (needs the provider's own file) can answer this for a historic period, so the route is **reconstructed**: cash lodged in lumps days later, card net of commission and the GST on it, UPI gross on its own cycle. The control the module stands on — **a commission rate is declared, never derived.** A rate computed as `(gross − banked) / gross` makes every shortfall commission by definition, so the check agrees perfectly at any figure; a test demonstrates a 60,000 hole reconciling to zero under a fitted rate, and the same input refused under `derived_from_the_difference`. **Cash is the dangerous direction** — the only tender a person carries, and the only difference with no counterparty who will ever chase it, so it carries its own figure plus the peak standing unlodged. **An unexplained credit is an exception, not a bonus**: migrated as revenue it overstates turnover and the tax due. The statement's span is read off its header, not inferred from its lines, and must run **past** the period end or the last day's batch is missing while the dates look matched. `provesSalesWereComplete` is typed as the literal `false`. Tested in `tests/unit/migration-banking-verification.test.ts` |
| Supplier reconciliation (§34, OB-06, MG-06) | `packages/migration/src/supplier-reconciliation.ts` | The external check that needs **nobody's goodwill** — the supplier keeps their own ledger and will confirm it, because they want paying. Two ledgers with no shared key that were never meant to agree at a point in time, so the job is telling a **timing difference** from a real one: within the window a payment in transit is the ordinary explanation, and past it, it is not. An `amount_differs` item is **never** timing, because both sides hold the document. Three rules that do not bend: **the dangerous direction is theirs** — an invoice only on their statement is a liability we would migrate as zero, so it is called out separately and sorted first; **nothing is netted**, because an unapplied payment and an unrecorded credit of the same size produce a clean zero that hides both; and **signs are mirrored deliberately** — every item carries its contribution to *theirs minus ours*, so the items sum to the headline difference and a reversed side cannot balance at twice the true figure. A supplier who never replied is named, not counted as agreeing. Tested in `tests/unit/migration-supplier-reconciliation.test.ts` |
| Export completeness (§34, OB-06, MG-01/05, P-08) | `packages/migration/src/completeness.ts` | The failure that RECONCILES PERFECTLY — a paginated grid exports one page, the file parses cleanly, its own grand total agrees to the paisa, and it holds 4,000 of 41,200 products. Four signals in order of trust: a row count taken off the screen; **`Page N of M`, which works when nobody wrote anything down**; the end marker, available only for a file that would have had one; and sequence density, deduped so repeats cannot inflate it. **Refused, not flagged.** `unverifiable` is a distinct verdict from `complete` — a file with no signals has failed to be caught, not passed. `compareDoubleKeyed` is Route D's control. Tested in `tests/unit/migration-completeness.test.ts` |
| Self-extraction round trip (§34, OB-06, MG-05) | `packages/migration/src/render-report.ts` | Renders a KNOWN dataset back into a printed report — banner, page breaks, repeated headers, department subtotals, Indian digit grouping — so the parser is measured against ground truth rather than against whether the answer looks plausible. `checkRoundTrip` names the product that went missing rather than counting rows short, and compares as a MULTISET so a lost second location cannot hide behind a still-present id. Proven end to end in `tests/integration/we-get-it-out-ourselves.test.ts`; tested in `tests/unit/migration-render-report.test.ts` |
| Legacy report parsing (§34, OB-06, §29.1) | `packages/migration/src/report-parser.ts` | What these systems ACTUALLY export — a printed page in a spreadsheet. Finds the header rather than assuming line 1; classifies subtotals FIRST so a group total is never counted as data (the misread that doubles a department); **Indian digit grouping** (4,12,000 is twelve lakh); **no float anywhere** — the decimal part is parsed as text, because `parseFloat(x)*100` turns 19.99 into 1998.9999999999998; `CR` read as negative so supplier balances keep their sign; a lone `-` read as nil. Nothing dropped silently, banner rows included. Tested in `tests/unit/migration-report-parser.test.ts` |
| Legacy self-extraction (§34, OB-06) | `packages/migration/src/extraction.ts` | **MG-01/02/06 without vendor cooperation** — four routes ranked by what they structurally lose, re-keying refused as a migration source because it cannot be re-run; and the control the approach stands on: a domain verified only against the system it came from is **refused by name**, so every total is proved against outside evidence (bank, filed returns, supplier statement, physical count). Procedure in `docs/runbooks/legacy-self-extraction.md`; tested in `tests/unit/migration-extraction.test.ts` |
| Migration engine (§34) | `packages/migration/` (`synthetic`, `discovery`, `mapping`, `cleaning`, `trial`, `reconcile`, `history`, `cutover`) | **MG-01…12 built and gated** — Stage 11 rehearsal against a synthetic legacy dataset with ten planted fault kinds, all found by identity; `docs/evidence/stage-11-the-old-shop-arrives-whole.md` |
| Threat & privacy model (§35) | `docs/security/threat-privacy-model.md` | trust boundaries, STRIDE, PRV, QG-06 |
| Security controls (SEC-02/03/12, PRV-03/05/08) | `tests/security/` (`access-control-sweep`, `separation-of-duties`, `data-protection`) | The **deny** path, swept rather than sampled: 240 access combinations against an oracle computed independently of the implementation; every self-authorisation point in the product in one list, each proven to allow with two people and refuse with one; card-data allowlist; erasure per category with the statute named. Found and fixed: `minimisePii` was a **blocklist wearing an allowlist's name** and passed `aadhaar_number` straight through; `AccessControl` held role permission lists by reference |
| Accessibility engine (NFR-07/13) | `packages/a11y/` (`contrast`, `signals`) | WCAG 2.2 AA contrast as ONE implementation for the product — fixed a shipped rounding defect that passed a failing 4.48:1 grey as 4.57:1; colour never the only signal (label + icon + announcement returned together, no `toneOf` helper exists); touch targets against both the WCAG 24px floor and the design system's 44px bar; the ≤3-interaction budget as an assertable number; focus order with every issue named at once |
| Performance budgets (§32/NFR-02/QG-05) | `tests/performance/` (`harness`, `pos-hot-path`, `sync-and-endurance`) | SHAPE not speed: scan proven O(1) at 100× catalogue, with a **tripwire that fires on a deliberate linear scan**; basket linear in its own lines; commit runs with the network globals removed (hard rule #1); outbox flat at 100× queue depth and 72h of trading; backlog drains in order exactly once. `certifiedHardwareGate()` records the six items that genuinely need EX-09 — `certifiesTheTarget` is typed as the literal `false` |
| Field-level data dictionary | `db/data-dictionary/` | six Store-Core domains, field level |

| Catalogue pack ASSEMBLY + SIGNED PUBLISH on the cloud (slice 2) | M03 → M05 → the lane / §31 / P-08 — folds the four cloud master-data stores (product master M03-FR-01, price lists M05, barcode register M03-FR-02, tax-class rate schedules M03-FR-03/A6) into ONE snapshot for a store via the tested `buildCatalogueSnapshot`. `GET /v1/catalogue/pack/preview?storeId=&asOf=` shows what the pack WOULD contain + which products are LEFT OUT and why (no_price/no_tax_class/price_above_mrp; gated `catalogue.pack.read`). **The SIGNED publish `POST /v1/catalogue/pack` now BUILDS FROM this fold** (via the shared `assembleCatalogueSnapshot`) for a `{ storeId, asOf }` — so an authored + priced + barcoded + taxed product reaches the lane on the signed pack it reads over `GET /v1/catalogue/pack`, and a product with no price/tax is never shipped unpriceable; the shrink check still refuses a pack much smaller than the shop without acknowledgement. **Still open:** no operator SCREEN to author a product (API-only), the §28 reversible merge-write, and `EmbeddedBarcodeRule`s (weight/price-embedded) not yet sourced on the cloud (register codes carried as 'standard'). | `services/catalogue/src/catalogue-preview.ts` (`assembleCatalogueSnapshot`), `services/catalogue/src/index.ts` (publish route), `services/api/src/adapters.ts` (`catalogueAdapter.buildSnapshot`, `cataloguePreviewAdapter`) | `tests/integration/catalogue-pack-preview.test.ts` (3), `tests/integration/catalogue-pack-publish.test.ts` (3: authored product on the SIGNED pack priced+taxed; unpriced left off; no-store refused), `tests/unit/service-catalogue.test.ts` (30, publish invariants) |
| Product-authoring SCREEN → cloud delivery (ADR-0013 slices 1–7: classifier + delivery + review-queue screen + shell/edge wiring + central-boundary re-checks & audit + barcode-assignment leg + browser E2E matrix) | M03-FR-01/03 / ADR-0013 / P-01 · P-03 · P-04 · P-08 / hard rule #2 · #4 · #6 · #10 — the owner chose (18 Aug) that a queued product publish is delivered by the SIGNED-IN OPERATOR as themselves, never a background service identity (only the owner role holds `catalogue.pack.publish`; the sync relay holds only `pos.sale.sync`). The review-queue classifier re-evaluates each queued item from the operator's CURRENT session/permissions/tenant — NEVER the queued snapshot — into `pending/ready/validation_failed/approval_required/conflict/published/permanently_refused`: a cross-tenant item is refused outright, a stale/absent grant routes rather than publishes, a creator who has since lost the authority is routed for reassignment, a conflict is surfaced (never last-write-wins), and a bulk/sensitive publish needs a fresh re-auth. Pure/deterministic; nothing here publishes (delivery is a separate operator action — no auto-publish). **Slice 2 (delivery):** `deliverReadyPublishes` sends ONLY the `ready` items through an injected OPERATOR-SESSION port (their own auth, never a service token) to the idempotent publish route, and transitions the durable outbox on each result — accepted (2xx) → acknowledged, retryable (timeout/5xx/429) → left pending + attempt counted, rejected (a 4xx) → dead-lettered visibly (hard rule #6). A **409 is a REAL conflict → rejected/dead-lettered** (`sku_already_in_use` / `barcode_already_assigned`), never an idempotent replay — the kernel returns the first response's cached status for a replay, so a 409 that reaches here is always a genuine clash a person must resolve (this corrected slice 2's earlier edge-transport-mirrored assumption that a 409 meant "already had it"). Still no auto-publish (a person triggers it). **Slice 3 (the operator SCREEN — session model):** a tested, DOM-free `createProductPublishReviewSession` presents the whole outbox (every item via `all()`, so a dead-lettered refusal and an already-published item are VISIBLE too — hard rule #6, not just the pending ones) with each item's live state as a word + icon (colour is never the only signal), the ones needing a person FIRST then oldest-queued first (control-by-exception), a per-exception plain-English "what to do" line, and bilingual EN/TA copy; it refuses to review a queue for a screen not told who is using it (hard rule #4); the `deliver()` method is a SEPARATE explicit action that delegates to slice 2 (only the ready keys are sent; nothing auto-publishes on view/login); `presentDeliveryReport` folds a result into one glanceable status (ok all-sent / degraded held / error refused). **Slice 4 (the SHELL + edge wiring):** the visible screen (`web/product-publish-review.html` + `.js`, opens offline via the shared web-erp service worker, sample stand-in when told nothing) draws the rows and a **Publish button that runs only on an explicit click** (never on load); `browser-entry` boots it over the SAME device-backed catalogue outbox the Save commits to (so what was queued is what is reviewed) with a **concrete operator-session delivery port** — `fetch(POST /v1/catalogue/products/:id/publish, credentials:'same-origin')` carrying the operator's OWN auth cookie, never a service token, idempotent on the command key, network/timeout → retryable; the store box serves it as a registered screen (`SCREENS`/`APP_SHELL`/`GLOBAL_FOR`/`payloadFor` + `PackProductPublishReviewPolicy`) feeding only the operator's CURRENT context (permissions/userId, re-read each render — NOT a queued snapshot; the cloud route re-checks on delivery), and the menu offers it gated on `catalogue.pack.publish`. **Slice 5 (central-boundary re-checks + audit — control 9/10):** the cloud publish route (`POST /v1/catalogue/products/:id/publish`) trusts NO client verdict — it re-runs the tested `publishProduct` compliance gate (mandatory fields, category, HSN/tax class, MRP/UOM, a regulated item's safety content) AND adds the one re-check a single offline device cannot do: **SKU uniqueness across the whole tenant** — a SKU already held by a DIFFERENT product is refused `409 sku_already_in_use` (nothing overwritten, hard rule #2), while re-publishing the SAME product under its own SKU is a legitimate new version, never a self-clash. **Audit attribution (control 10) is already provided by the kernel** — the request pipeline writes a SHA-256-sealed, append-only `audit_log` row (per-tenant hash chain, FND-02) for every write AND every refusal, naming the AUTHENTICATED `userId` (never a client value), path, status and permission — so publish/refuse/retry are on the record by construction. **Slice 6 (the barcode-assignment leg):** delivering ONE command now means publishing the product master AND assigning each of its barcodes — the publish route takes product+categories only, so the command carries each barcode as `{code, kind}` (the symbology is a real authoring fact — a weight/price-embedded code parses differently at the till — never guessed at delivery), and `deliverOnePublish` sequences the operator-session POSTs: product FIRST (a barcode names a product that may not exist yet), then each barcode on the one-code-one-item route, collapsing the multi-step result into ONE status the outbox transitions on — a real barcode conflict (`409 barcode_already_assigned`) → dead-lettered for a person, a transient barcode failure → whole command held + retried (re-publish is idempotent and an already-assigned barcode is a 201 no-op, so a retry converges), all-in → acknowledged; each barcode's idempotency key is derived from the command key so no retry can double-assign. **Slice 7 (the browser E2E matrix — the whole chain, verified in a real Chromium against a stub cloud):** a headless-browser test seeds the device-backed outbox with a queued publish, injects the operator context, and drives the actual screen — (a) an authorised operator presses Publish and the product-master POST THEN the barcode POST both arrive (product before barcode, each keyed off the command), the result strip says "published", and the outbox item clears to acknowledged; (b) a no-permission operator's Publish button is disabled and NOTHING is sent (never publishes under a grant not held); (c) a barcode already owned by another product (409) dead-letters the whole command, visible for a person. Browser-gated skip like the offline suite. **This E2E — the first actual browser RUN of the review screen — also caught and fixed a real defect the CI-skipped e2e had hidden: `product-publish-review.js` was missing from the service-worker SHELL cache, so the screen did NOT truly open offline; added to `sw.js` SHELL + cache bumped v13→v14. | Product-authoring SCREEN → cloud write path (slices 1–2: durable command + Save wired) | M03-FR-01/03 / §31 / P-01 / P-02 — the catalogue screen already VALIDATES a product against the tested compliance rules; this is the Save that reaches the shared truth. Like every ERP write action (the GST-returns/write-off pattern), Save commits a deterministic, deduplicated `ProductPublishRequested` command to the offline OUTBOX rather than calling the network inline (a click survives a reload; the drain is idempotent). The tested `publishProduct` runs FIRST, so a product that would not validate never even queues (every missing field named at once) and a draft is promoted to 'new' before it is sent. Dedupe is by CONTENT (a double-click/reload collapses; an edit is a distinct command). **Slice 2 (Save wired):** `catalogue-session.requestPublish` queues via an OPTIONAL injected outbox port (`no_outbox` said plainly when absent — never a silent drop); `browser-entry` opens a DEVICE-backed outbox (`sre.catalogue-outbox`, degrades to in-memory + surfaces `catalogueStorageProblem`, P-08) and the shell's Publish button now commits the publish to it — no network from the screen. **Follow-on slice 3:** the sync-agent DRAIN that POSTs the queued command to `POST /v1/catalogue/products/:id/publish` (+ barcodes/tax). | `apps/web-erp/src/catalogue-publish-command.ts`, `apps/web-erp/src/catalogue-session.ts` (`requestPublish`), `apps/web-erp/src/browser-entry.ts` (`openCatalogueOutbox`), `apps/web-erp/web/catalogue.js` (Publish button) | `tests/unit/erp-catalogue-publish-command.test.ts` (4), `tests/unit/erp-catalogue-session.test.ts` (+3: requestPublish queues via outbox; double-click collapses; `no_outbox` when absent) |

| Supplier portal — isolation, compliance & submissions | M24-FR-01/02/03/04 / §35 / P-04 / hard rule #6 — the only place someone OUTSIDE the business logs in, so the partner id is taken from the authenticated session and never from the request, and a request naming another partner returns a recorded `not_your_data` security event rather than an empty list (no function in the module accepts a partner id from a payload, asserted by test); the tenant boundary holds even for the same partner id; compliance is checked AT THE ACTION rather than on a nightly sweep because that gap is exactly where an expired supplier gets a purchase order, and an unverified document counts as missing since an unverified licence is a photograph somebody uploaded; nothing a supplier submits takes effect on its own — a catalogue, RFQ response or claim lands for review; a statement is built from named buckets and cross-checked a second way so an uncategorised kind cannot vanish from a balance, a dispute is shown separately, and a missing grant reports a permission answer rather than a balance of zero; refusals are audited as loudly as successes and repeat probing is surfaced | `packages/supplier-portal/src/portal.ts` | `tests/unit/supplier-portal.test.ts` (26) |
| Workforce — rosters, gated tasks, incentives & SOPs | M25-FR-01/02/03/04 / §28 / §29.1 / P-04 — a roster gap is NAMED with the hour ("Sunday 06:00 has NOBODY rostered as opener") rather than averaged into a coverage percentage nobody acts on, and a leaver still in the grid counts as no cover; a lapsed certification blocks the TASK and not the person, returning what they may still do, because blocking the person is how a shop routes around the system on a busy Saturday and a control people route around is not a control; blocking and non-blocking checklist items are separated so the one item that mattered is not ticked with the rest, and an unsigned checklist is a list not a record; incentives are exact integer arithmetic and a missed target pays NOTHING, since paying 96% for 96% redefines the target; labour cost is REPORTED, never enforced — a system that refuses a fourth cashier creates queues at Diwali, and no function exists that could refuse a roster on cost (asserted by test); acknowledging SOP v3 is not acknowledging v5 | `packages/workforce/src/workforce.ts` | `tests/unit/workforce.test.ts` (31) |
| B2B document chain — quote to tax invoice | M22-FR-02 / M01-FR-02 / M23 — each document is derived from the one before it and never from the one two steps back, because the chain drifting is how an invoice for 40 cases follows a van that carried 38; a quotation is non-committing and draws NO number when it refuses, since a gap in a tax series is a question from an assessing officer with no good answer; conversion is at the QUOTED price or refused outright rather than re-priced quietly at the invoice, and credit control is not optional; a proforma carries `taxClaimable: false` because input credit claimed against it is claimed against a document never filed; a challan carries what actually LEFT and over-delivery is refused; the tax invoice is built from the CHALLANS so a partial delivery bills partially — billing the ordered quantity is an overcharge with a tax invoice attached to it; the chain check names goods gone out with no claim on them | `packages/b2b/src/documents.ts` | `tests/unit/b2b-documents.test.ts` (21) |
| B2B portal, ageing & collections | M22-FR-04 / M23 / ADR-0003 / P-05 / hard rule #3 — the second outside login, scoped from the session with a cross-customer request refused as a security event; ageing runs from the DUE DATE and not the invoice date, because ageing from issue makes every account on terms look delinquent and then the report is ignored along with the genuinely overdue account; a payment is ALLOCATED to named invoices, never absorbed into a balance, with an overpayment held visibly unapplied; a disputed invoice is outstanding but never chaseable; stopping supply is RECOMMENDED and needs a person, because date arithmetic must not cut off a school on the morning of a function; the portal is reconciled to finance exactly and with its sign | `packages/b2b/src/collections.ts` | `tests/unit/b2b-collections.test.ts` (20) |
| Facilities — assets, downtime & energy | M26-FR-01/04 / §35 / D14 — criticality is a property of the ASSET not of the alert, so critical assets come back in their own list rather than merely sorted higher (a list where the cold room sits beside the shelf trolley is one where the cold room is missed); an expired AMC is reported against WHAT IT PROTECTS in money, because "AMC-14 expired" gets ignored and "₹80,000 of stock depends on it" gets renewed; a breakdown call is not preventive maintenance; downtime is measured from when it BROKE and unreported minutes are a number of their own, since an hour reported four hours late is a five-hour exposure; an energy figure states how much of it was ESTIMATED, because it will be quoted in a decision about buying a chiller. **WIRED on the cloud surface 7 Aug** — assets registered, services logged (breakdown does not reset the clock), downtime recorded and summarised from failure, energy readings kept with their source; health/downtime/energy read through the real API with real RBAC | `packages/facilities/src/assets.ts`, `services/platform/src/facilities-assets.ts`, `services/api/src/adapters.ts` | `tests/unit/facilities.test.ts` (39), `tests/integration/facilities-assets.test.ts` |
| Facilities — equipment & power monitoring | M26-FR-02 / D14 / M10 / P-08 — M10 assesses the BATCH, this assesses the EQUIPMENT: the store's habit is to probe a few batches and the room is what actually fails, so a breach names every batch in it and its value and holds them all, including the ones nobody probed; a sensor that has gone QUIET is a fault and not a pass, because the probe that fell out of the room three weeks ago has read as "no alerts" ever since — `no_data` and `stale` both hold; IoT is readiness rather than a dependency, so a hand-written log sheet and a sensor feed are assessed identically with the source recorded; power is assessed by WHAT IT PROTECTS, with unprotected minutes counted from the mains failure rather than the generator attempt because the stock does not care which equipment let it down. **WIRED on the cloud surface 7 Aug** — a range and current contents are set per equipment, readings arrive from sensor/manual-probe/log-sheet, power events recorded; `GET …/equipment/:id` returns the assessment (breach holds every batch, silence is `no_data`/`stale`) and `GET …/power` names the unprotected critical assets — all through the real API with real RBAC | `packages/facilities/src/monitoring.ts`, `services/platform/src/facilities-monitoring.ts`, `services/api/src/adapters.ts` | `tests/unit/facilities.test.ts` (39), `tests/integration/facilities-monitoring.test.ts` |
| Facilities — safety schedules, incidents & evidence | M26-FR-03/04 / P-03 / §28 / hard rule #6 — a tick without evidence is REFUSED rather than accepted with a note, since an accepted-with-a-note task shows green and green is what everybody reads; self-verification of a safety check is a signature against nothing; a missed compliance task ESCALATES by itself to a named person, because a task that quietly rolls into tomorrow's list forever is invisible precisely because nothing ever goes red — and cleaning is deliberately not compliance-linked so the fire check is not buried among forty mop-the-aisle alerts; an incident cannot be closed with no corrective action, by its own reporter, or (when reportable) with no statutory notification, because closing it internally is what makes everybody stop thinking about it; the evidence pack says plainly whether it would survive an inspection and names every gap. **WIRED on the cloud surface 7 Aug** — schedules/tasks/overdue and now incidents: `POST /v1/facilities/incidents/:id` raises one, `…/close` runs `closeIncident` (refuses no-action / no-evidence-for-serious / self-close / unreported-reportable, the closed record superseding the open one), and `GET /v1/facilities/evidence` runs `buildComplianceEvidence` — every gap named, `presentable` false while any remains | `packages/facilities/src/schedules.ts`, `services/platform/src/facilities.ts`, `services/api/src/adapters.ts` | `tests/unit/facilities.test.ts` (39), `tests/integration/facilities-schedules.test.ts`, `tests/integration/facilities-incidents.test.ts` |
| Concession & shop-in-shop | M27-FR-01/02/03/04 / §28 / P-04 / hard rules #2, #5 — ownership is a property of the STOCK and the valuation asks, because concession stock on our shelves swallowed into our valuation overstates the balance sheet, the insurance schedule and the tax position at once and always looks about right; what was excluded is named and valued, since a quiet exclusion is as dangerous as a quiet inclusion; store staff may see and (under contract) sell concession stock but never adjust or write it off; revenue share is exact BigInt arithmetic on sales net of refunds and `higher_of_both` is the HIGHER, never the sum; the money a concession sale takes was never our revenue — it is held on their behalf and discharged at settlement, and a banking difference is a valued exception; the deposit is stated but NEVER netted, projected from movements with an unapproved forfeit still a liability; lapsed insurance blocks new sales and every blocker is reported at once | `packages/concession/src/concession.ts` | `tests/unit/concession.test.ts` (30) |
| Scrap & recycling sales | M28-FR-02 / M23 / hard rule #6 — the one revenue stream in most shops with no paperwork and no controls, and because nobody knows what a month of cardboard is worth nobody can tell ₹4,000 from ₹12,000; the control is not suspicion, it is making the number EXIST; proceeds that have not reached the books are named as off-books cash whatever anyone intended; an unevidenced sale is FLAGGED and not refused, because refusing pushes the transaction back outside the system which is the problem we started with; rate drift is measured against the shop's OWN running average rather than a configured expectation that would be wrong within a quarter, needs enough history first, and asks about the RATE rather than the person; e-waste and used oil require a registered recycler | `packages/waste/src/scrap.ts` | `tests/unit/waste-sustainability.test.ts` (29) |
| Packaging, carry bags & reusable crates | M28-FR-03 / M12 / M05 / P-01 — a bag charge is a VISIBLE priced line or it does not exist, because an amount added to a total without a line is a consumer complaint the shop loses and showing it properly costs nothing; where the tenant has not enabled it there is NO line rather than a line worth nothing; the charge is pure arithmetic over the price pack the lane already holds, so it works with the internet down; a reusable crate is an asset IN CIRCULATION rather than a consumable, so 118 crates unaccounted for is a number instead of a feeling and the shop stops buying 400 again every year; a negative position is reported NEGATIVE rather than clamped, because the negative is the only evidence that a goods-in was never entered | `packages/waste/src/packaging.ts` | `tests/unit/waste-sustainability.test.ts` (29) |
| Waste, energy & sustainability reporting | M28-FR-04 / M26-FR-04 / P-08 / P-03 — a store reports "waste down 18%" and waste is not down, RECORDING is down: the one manager who logged every damaged crate went on leave, and six months later the shop believes it controls a problem it has stopped measuring; so the number carries its COVERAGE on the face of the report rather than in a footnote, below 80% it is `not_comparable` in those words, and the departments that did not report are named; waste is valued and broken down by source and department because "₹2,40,000 of waste" is unactionable and "₹1,80,000 of it is fresh produce expiring on the shelf" is an ordering decision; a period comparison REFUSES to call a fall an improvement when coverage moved — "we cannot tell" is worth more than a number the owner will repeat | `packages/waste/src/sustainability.ts` | `tests/unit/waste-sustainability.test.ts` (29) |
| **Stage 16 gate — beyond the till** | Stage 16 gate / M22 / M24–M28 / QG-06 / §28 — one trading day followed against real PostgreSQL through the six things a hypermarket does besides selling at a till: a school quoted, converted at the quoted price and refused a late re-price, with an invoice that follows the VAN (₹38,062.50) and not the order (₹82,950), a 40-day-old invoice aged as 10 days overdue, a queried invoice never chased while the same account undisputed would face a human stop-supply decision, and a payment allocated to named invoices; a supplier shown only their own rows with a rival request refused AND recorded, three attempts surfaced as probing, an expired licence stopping an ASN at the door and a renewed one letting it through while a catalogue still needs review, and a statement that reconciles with its dispute separate; ₹4,00,000 of a jeweller's gold kept out of our valuation, our own manager refused a write-off on it, the higher of rent and revenue share charged, ₹1,800 of banking difference valued, an unapproved forfeit still a liability, and the counter shut on lapsed insurance with every reason at once; a cold room holding ₹1,840 of stock including what nobody probed, a silent probe treated as a fault, and 47 unprotected minutes counted from the mains failure; "Sunday 06:00 has NOBODY rostered as opener", the deli counter blocked but not Raj, an old SOP signature rejected, 96% of target paying nothing, and one fire check escalated above 40 mop-the-aisle tasks; a reportable injury refused closure three times and a compliance pack declared not presentable; scrap rate drift asked of the rate not the person, a carry bag billed as a visible line, 118 crates never returned, and an 18% "improvement" in waste reported as `not_comparable`; then six events banked idempotently and the database refusing DELETE and UPDATE.
